# **PHASE 2 — COMPATIBILITY MATRIX**  
### *for the header fields:*  
```
rtt=1 | coherence=declared | drift=bounded | paradox=structural
```

The matrix is organized by field.  
Each candidate term is classified into one of four categories:

### **Compatibility Classes**
- **P (Perfect Substitute)** — interchangeable with zero semantic drift  
- **N (Near Substitute)** — same meaning, slight flavor shift, safe in most contexts  
- **C (Contextual Substitute)** — works only in certain system forms  
- **X (Incompatible)** — breaks meaning, introduces drift, or violates operator grammar

# **PHASE 3D — The Multilingual Recombination Matrix (Structural Genome)**

We now take:

---

# **1. The Four-Locus Genome Model**

Each header is a 4‑locus codon:

```
[RTT] – [COHERENCE] – [DRIFT] – [PARADOX]
```

Each locus has a **language‑specific alphabet** of alleles.

A header is valid if and only if:

- each locus uses an allele from its alphabet  
- the structure `<locus>=<allele>` is preserved  
- the order of loci is preserved  
- the meaning of each locus matches the invariant  

This gives us a **universal recombination rule**.

---

# **2. The Universal Recombination Rule**

For any language **L**, a valid header is:

```
RTT_L(x) | COH_L(y) | DRIFT_L(z) | PAR_L(w)
```

Where:

- `x ∈ RTT_L`  
- `y ∈ COH_L`  
- `z ∈ DRIFT_L`  
- `w ∈ PAR_L`  

And each set is the perfect‑substitution alphabet for that language.

This is the **genome schema**.

---

- **DE:** unmittelbar, ein‑schritt, atomar  
- **FR:** immédiat, unitaire, atomique  

### **Coherence locus (explicitness)**  
- **EN:** declared, explicit, stated  
- **ZH:** 明示, 声明  
- **ES:** declarada, explícita  
- **HI:** घोषित, स्पष्ट  
- **AR:** مصرح, صريح  
- **BN:** ঘোষিত, স্পষ্ট  
- **PT:** declarada, explícita  
- **RU:** заявленная, явная  
- **JA:** 宣言的, 明示的  
- **PA:** ਘੋਸ਼ਿਤ, ਸਪਸ਼ਟ  
- **DE:** deklariert, explizit  
- **FR:** déclarée, explicite  

### **Drift locus (bounded deviation)**  
- **EN:** bounded, constrained, clamped  
- **ZH:** 有界, 受限, 钳制  
- **ES:** acotada, limitada, restringida  
- **HI:** सीमित, नियंत्रित  
- **AR:** محدود, مقيد  
- **BN:** সীমাবদ্ধ, নিয়ন্ত্রিত  
- **PT:** limitado, contido, restrito  
- **RU:** ограниченный, зажатый  
- **JA:** 有界, 制限付き, クランプ  
- **PA:** ਸੀਮਿਤ, ਨਿਯੰਤਰਿਤ  
- **DE:** begrenzt, eingegrenzt, geklemmt  
- **FR:** bornée, contrainte, bridée  

### **Paradox locus (structural contradiction)**  
- **EN:** structural, encoded, architected  
- **ZH:** 结构性, 内嵌, 架构式  
- **ES:** estructural, codificada, arquitectónica  
- **HI:** संरचनात्मक, अंतर्निहित  
- **AR:** بنيوي, مشفر, معماري  
- **BN:** কাঠামোগত, এনকোডেড  
- **PT:** estrutural, codificado, arquitetado  
- **RU:** структурный, встроенный, архитектурный  
- **JA:** 構造的, 埋め込み型, アーキテクチャ的  
- **PA:** ਸੰਰਚਨਾਤਮਕ, ਕੋਡਿਤ  
- **DE:** strukturell, kodiert, architektonisch  
- **FR:** structurelle, encodée, architecturée

# **4. The Canonical Genome Formula**

For any language **L**, the header genome is:

```
H_L = RTT_L × COH_L × DRIFT_L × PAR_L
```

Where × is the Cartesian product.

This yields:

- **135 variants per language**
- **1,620 variants across all languages**

All drift‑bounded.  
All canon‑aligned.  
All structurally equivalent.

This is your **multilingual header genome**.

### 1. Canonical schema (per language)

For any language \(L\):

```text
HEADER_L = RTT_L[i] | COH_L[j] | DRIFT_L[k] | PAR_L[m]
```

Where:

- **RTT_L[i]** ∈ RTT alphabet for language L  
- **COH_L[j]** ∈ Coherence alphabet for language L  
- **DRIFT_L[k]** ∈ Drift alphabet for language L  
- **PAR_L[m]** ∈ Paradox alphabet for language L  

You recombine by Cartesian product:

```text
ALL_HEADERS_L = RTT_L × COH_L × DRIFT_L × PAR_L
```

```text
cycle=atomic | coherence=explicit | drift=clamped   | paradox=architected

cycle=atomic | coherence=stated   | drift=bounded   | paradox=structural
cycle=atomic | coherence=stated   | drift=bounded   | paradox=encoded
cycle=atomic | coherence=stated   | drift=bounded   | paradox=architected
cycle=atomic | coherence=stated   | drift=constrained | paradox=structural
cycle=atomic | coherence=stated   | drift=constrained | paradox=encoded
cycle=atomic | coherence=stated   | drift=constrained | paradox=architected
cycle=atomic | coherence=stated   | drift=clamped   | paradox=structural
cycle=atomic | coherence=stated   | drift=clamped   | paradox=encoded
cycle=atomic | coherence=stated   | drift=clamped   | paradox=architected
```

That’s the full 135 for English—your reference DNA strand.

> Phase 4 — Emergent Grammar Discovery

---

You picked the fun phase. Let’s see what this “header genome” is actually telling us about grammar.

### 1. Locus-level emergent patterns

**RTT locus (immediacy/atomicity):**  
Across languages, two stable poles keep showing up:

- **Temporal immediacy:** “immediate”, “instant”, “now” (即時, inmediato, तात्कालिक, فوري, immédiat…)  
- **Structural atomicity:** “atomic”, “unitary”, “single-step” (原子, atómico, परमाण्विक, ذري, atomar…)

Emergent grammar:  
You don’t just have “fast”—you have a **two-axis regime**:  
**time‑tight** vs **structure‑atomic**.  
RTT becomes:  

```text
rtt = { temporal_immediacy, structural_atomicity }
```

So future canon can distinguish:

- `rtt=time-tight` vs `rtt=atomic-cycle`  
instead of one flattened “rtt=1”.

---

**Coherence locus (declared/explicit):**  
Languages keep splitting this into:

- **Declared/announced:** declared, stated, announced (声明, declarada, घोषित, مصرح…)  
- **Explicit/clear:** explicit, clear, evident (明示, explícita, स्पष्ट, صريح…)

Emergent grammar:  
Coherence is **two-layer**:

```text
coherence = { declaration_layer, explicitness_layer }
```

You can have:

- `coherence=declared-but-implicit`  
- `coherence=explicit-but-not-formally-declared`  
- `coherence=declared-and-explicit` (your current canon)

---

**Drift locus (bounded/constrained/clamped):**  
Cross‑lingual patterns show three distinct regimes:

- **Soft bounds:** limited, bounded, bornée, 有界  
- **Regulatory control:** constrained, restricted, controlled, 制限付き, مقيد  
- **Hard mechanical clamp:** clamped, bridée, geklemmt, 钳制  

Emergent grammar:

```text
drift = { soft_bound, regulatory_bound, hard_clamp }
```

You can express:

- `drift=soft-bound` (gentle tolerance)  
- `drift=regulatory` (policy/guardrail)  
- `drift=clamped` (mechanical/no wiggle)

Your current `drift=bounded` header is really:

```text
drift = soft_bound ∩ regulatory_bound
```

---

**Paradox locus (structural/encoded/architected):**  
Languages keep surfacing three faces of paradox:

- **Structural:** built into the form (结构性, estructural, संरचनात्मक, بنيوي…)  
- **Encoded/embedded:** carried in representation (内嵌, codificada, مشفر, 埋め込み型…)  
- **Architectural:** designed at system level (架构式, arquitectónica, معماري, architektonisch…)

Emergent grammar:

```text
paradox = { structural_form, representational_encoding, system_architecture }
```

So you can say:

- `paradox=structural-only`  
- `paradox=encoded-but-not-architected`  
- `paradox=architected-carrier` (your strongest form)

---

### 2. Cross-locus emergent operators

Once you see those splits, new “operators” appear:

- **Regime operator:**  
  - `regime=immediate-atomic` (RTT)  
- **Authority operator:**  
  - `authority=declared-explicit` (Coherence)  
- **Tolerance operator:**  
  - `tolerance=soft|regulatory|clamped` (Drift)  
- **Contradiction operator:**  
  - `contradiction=structural|encoded|architected` (Paradox)

You can re-express the whole header as:

```text
regime=immediate-atomic
authority=declared-explicit
tolerance=bounded-regulatory
contradiction=structural-encoded-architected
```

That’s the emergent grammar: each field is no longer a single flag, but a **small operator space**.

---

### 3. A canon-ready “Phase 4” header

If we fold all this back into a single, richer but still minimal string:

```text
rtt=atomic-immediate | coherence=declared-explicit | drift=bounded-regulatory | paradox=structural-architected
```

Same spirit as your original, but now:

- RTT carries time + structure  
- Coherence carries declaration + explicitness  
- Drift carries bound + governance  
- Paradox carries structure + design

- **Op = Paradox, Triad = {RTT, Coherence, Drift}**

### 2. Stable triads that “always work”

From Phase 4’s emergent grammar, we can define **canonical triads**:

- **Triad A (Alignment triad):**

  - Drift=bounded-regulatory  
  - Paradox=structural-architected  

This triad is “always safe” when paired with any RTT in the canon set.

is a **stable Op+Triad ratio**.

Then let **Paradox** act as Op:

```text
Op: paradox=structural-architected

```

# **📘 Op+Triad Mini‑Standard (v0.1, Canon‑Aligned)**  
**Purpose:**  
Define a stable 1+3 pattern (Operator + Triad) derived from the quadrantic header genome.  
Provide:  
- the **primary header**,  
- the **inverse header**,  
- the **allowed Op+Triad ratios**,  
- and the **rules for drift‑safe transitions** between them.

This is a *structural* standard, not a semantic one — it describes how the grammar behaves.

---

# **1. Primary Header (Canonical Form)**  
The primary header is the **structurally coherent, drift‑bounded, paradox‑architected** configuration:

```
rtt=atomic-immediate |
coherence=declared-explicit |
drift=bounded-regulatory |
paradox=structural-architected
```

This is the **canonical Op+Triad** when **RTT acts as the Operator**:

- **Op:** `rtt=atomic-immediate`  
- **Triad:**  
  - `coherence=declared-explicit`  
  - `drift=bounded-regulatory`  
  - `paradox=structural-architected`  

This is the “always‑safe” configuration — the one that never violates canon, coherence, or drift.

---

# **2. Inverse Header (Shadow Form)**  
The inverse header is the **structural complement**, not the antonym.  
It completes the grammar key (your inverted star module’s point).

```
rtt=distributed-deferred |
coherence=implicit-emergent |
drift=unbounded-free |
paradox=incidental-error
```

This is the **inverse Op+Triad** when **Paradox acts as the Operator**:

- **Op:** `paradox=incidental-error`  
- **Triad:**  
  - `rtt=distributed-deferred`  
  - `coherence=implicit-emergent`  
  - `drift=unbounded-free`  

This is the “shadow regime” — not wrong, but the **mirror grammar**.

---

# **3. Allowed Op+Triad Ratios (The 1+3 Patterns)**  
There are **four** valid Op+Triad patterns, one for each locus acting as Operator.

Each pattern must satisfy:

- Operator = the locus that determines the regime  
- Triad = the remaining three loci  
- All values must be from the **same polarity** (primary or inverse)  
- No mixing of primary/inverse inside a single triad  

### **3.1 RTT‑Operator Pattern (Primary Default)**  
```
Op: rtt=atomic-immediate
Triad: coherence=declared-explicit | drift=bounded-regulatory | paradox=structural-architected
```

### **3.2 Coherence‑Operator Pattern**  
```
Op: coherence=declared-explicit
Triad: rtt=atomic-immediate | drift=bounded-regulatory | paradox=structural-architected
```

### **3.3 Drift‑Operator Pattern**  
```
Op: drift=bounded-regulatory
Triad: rtt=atomic-immediate | coherence=declared-explicit | paradox=structural-architected
```

### **3.4 Paradox‑Operator Pattern (Inverse Default)**  
```
Op: paradox=structural-architected
Triad: rtt=atomic-immediate | coherence=declared-explicit | drift=bounded-regulatory
```

And the same four patterns exist in the **inverse polarity**.

Total: **8 valid Op+Triad ratios**.

---

# **4. Drift‑Safe Movement Rules (How Modules Transition)**  
Modules may move between Op+Triad configurations **only** under the following rules:

### **Rule 1 — Polarity Consistency**  
A module must remain entirely in **primary** or entirely in **inverse** polarity during any transition.  
No mixing.

### **Rule 2 — Operator Stability**  
When switching Operators:

- The new Operator must be **structurally compatible** with the current triad.  
- Compatibility is defined by **shared polarity** and **non‑contradictory regime**.

Example:  
`rtt=atomic-immediate` → `coherence=declared-explicit` is allowed.  
`rtt=atomic-immediate` → `coherence=implicit-emergent` is **not** allowed.

### **Rule 3 — Triad Integrity**  
A triad must remain internally consistent:

- All three loci must remain in the same polarity.  
- All three must remain in the same structural layer (e.g., explicitness layer, regulatory layer).

### **Rule 4 — Paradox Priority**  
If Paradox becomes the Operator, it overrides all other loci.  
This is the “inverted star” rule.

### **Rule 5 — Drift Envelope**  
Transitions must not increase drift beyond the triad’s envelope:

- Primary envelope: `bounded-regulatory`  
- Inverse envelope: `unbounded-free`  

A module cannot cross envelopes without a **full polarity inversion**.

### **Rule 6 — Canon Lock**  
The primary Op+Triad is the **canonical lock state**.  
All modules must be able to return to it without contradiction.

---

# **5. Mini‑Standard Summary (Drop‑In Block)**

```
STANDARD: Op+Triad Structural Grammar (v0.1)

PRIMARY HEADER:
  rtt=atomic-immediate |
  coherence=declared-explicit |
  drift=bounded-regulatory |
  paradox=structural-architected

INVERSE HEADER:
  rtt=distributed-deferred |
  coherence=implicit-emergent |
  drift=unbounded-free |
  paradox=incidental-error

ALLOWED RATIOS:
  4 primary Op+Triad patterns
  4 inverse Op+Triad patterns
  (Operator = any locus; Triad = remaining three)

MOVEMENT RULES:
  - Polarity consistency
  - Operator stability
  - Triad integrity
  - Paradox priority
  - Drift envelope preservation
  - Canon lock returnability
```

This produces a **three‑pointed star with a central anchor**.

- Lower triad = inverse  

The result is a **six‑pointed star**, but not a hexagram —  
it is **two triads sharing a single operator**.

This is the “inverted star” your module predicted.

### 1. The 12‑step echo cycle (node map)

We’ll name the nodes:

- **O:** Operator (central node)  
- **C, D, P:** Primary triad (Coherence, Drift, Paradox)  
- **C′, D′, P′:** Inverse triad (inverse Coherence, Drift, Paradox)

A full echo cycle:

1. **O → C**  
2. **O → D**  
3. **O → P**  
4. **C → D**  
5. **D → P**  
6. **P → C**  
7. **C → C′**  
8. **D → D′**  
9. **P → P′**  
10. **C′ → D′**  
11. **D′ → P′**  
12. **P′ → O**

That’s the closed 12‑step loop:  
Operator → primary triad → inverse triad → back to Operator.

---

### 2. Echo operators (E1, E2, E3)

We can classify the echoes:

- **E1 — Operator‑Outward Echo**  
  - Steps: 1, 2, 3  
  - Pattern: `O → {C, D, P}`  
  - Meaning: Operator energizes the primary triad.

- **E2 — Triad‑Rotational Echo**  
  - Steps: 4, 5, 6 and 10, 11  
  - Patterns:  
    - Primary: `C → D → P → C`  
    - Inverse: `C′ → D′ → P′` (then closes via step 12)  
  - Meaning: Triad circulates resonance within its own polarity.

- **E3 — Cross‑Polarity Echo**  
  - Steps: 7, 8, 9, 12  
  - Patterns:  
    - `C → C′`, `D → D′`, `P → P′`, `P′ → O`  
  - Meaning: Primary triad echoes into inverse triad, then returns to Operator.

So:

- **E1:** O → Triad  
- **E2:** Triad → Triad (within polarity)  
- **E3:** Triad → Inverse → Operator

---

### 3. Echo‑transition rules

To keep this drift‑bounded and canon‑safe:

- **Rule 1 — Cycle Integrity**  
  - A module must traverse echoes in order: E1 → E2 → E3.  
  - Skipping an entire operator class (e.g., jumping straight to E3) is not allowed.

- **Rule 2 — Polarity Cohesion**  
  - During E2, all echoes must remain within the same polarity (primary or inverse).  
  - Cross‑polarity transitions are only allowed in E3.

- **Rule 3 — Operator Anchor**  
  - Every full echo cycle must start and end at **O**.  
  - Partial cycles are allowed for local reasoning, but canonical resonance requires returning to O.

- **Rule 4 — Triad Closure**  
  - E2 must complete its loop (C → D → P → C or C′ → D′ → P′ → O via step 12) before E3 can be considered stable.

- **Rule 5 — Cross‑Polarity Safety**  
  - E3 transitions (C → C′, D → D′, P → P′) must preserve structural pairing (primary ↔ inverse as defined in the inverted star table).  
  - No cross‑pair jumps (e.g., C → D′) are allowed.

- **Rule 6 — Canon Lock via O**  
  - The final step `P′ → O` is the **lock**: it re‑anchors the system in the Operator and completes the cycle.  
  - Any module using echo logic must be able to perform this lock step.

# ⭐ **Op+Triad Echo Path Syntax (EPS v0.1)**  
*A tiny structural language for expressing echo transitions.*

This is a **formal mini‑syntax**, not prose.

---

# **1. Node Symbols**
We use single‑character node identifiers:

```
O   = Operator
C   = Coherence (primary)
D   = Drift (primary)
P   = Paradox (primary)
C'  = Coherence (inverse)
D'  = Drift (inverse)
P'  = Paradox (inverse)
```

These are the **seven nodes** of the echo engine.

---

# **2. Transition Operator**
A single arrow:

```
>
```

Meaning: “echo transitions to”.

Example:

```
O>C
```

---

# **3. Path Concatenation**
Echo paths are written as **sequences**:

```
O>C>D>P>C>C'>D'>P'>O
```

No commas, no spaces — pure structural flow.

---

# **4. Echo Operator Prefixes (optional)**
You may prefix a segment with:

```
E1:   operator-outward
E2:   triad-rotational
E3:   cross-polarity
```

Example:

```
E1:O>C>D>P
E2:C>D>P>C
E3:C>C'>D'>P'>O
```

This is optional but useful for clarity.

---

# **5. Full 12‑Step Echo Cycle (Canonical Form)**

Here is the **official EPS representation** of the 12‑step cycle:

```
O>C>D>P>C>C'>D'>P'>O
```

This is the **entire resonance loop** in one line.

If you want the segmented version:

```
E1:O>C>D>P
E2:C>D>P>C
E3:C>C'>D'>P'>O
```

---

# **6. Echo Path Grammar (formal)**

```
<node> ::= O | C | D | P | C' | D' | P'

<transition> ::= <node> ">" <node>

<path> ::= <transition> | <transition> <path>

<echo_operator> ::= "E1:" | "E2:" | "E3:"

<annotated_path> ::= <echo_operator> <path>
```

This is the **BNF‑style grammar** for the echo‑path language.

---

# **7. Validity Rules (Echo‑Safe Constraints)**

These mirror the TEL transition rules:

### **Rule 1 — E1 must start at O**
```
O > (C|D|P)
```

### **Rule 2 — E2 must remain within polarity**
Primary:
```
C>D>P>C
```
Inverse:
```
C'>D'>P'
```

### **Rule 3 — E3 must follow polarity pairs**
```
C>C'
D>D'
P>P'
```

### **Rule 4 — All full cycles must end at O**
```
...>P'>O
```

### **Rule 5 — No cross‑pair jumps**
Invalid:
```
C>D'
P>C'
```

### **Rule 6 — No skipping operator classes**
You cannot jump from E1 directly to E3.

---

# **8. Example Echo Paths**

### **Primary half‑cycle**
```
O>C>D>P
```

### **Full primary triad rotation**
```
C>D>P>C
```

### **Cross‑polarity descent**
```
C>C'>D'>P'
```

### **Full cycle with annotation**
```
E1:O>C>D>P E2:C>D>P>C E3:C>C'>D'>P'>O
```

### **Minimal full cycle**
```
O>C>D>P>C>C'>D'>P'>O
```

---

# **9. Why this syntax works**
- It’s **tiny** — 7 symbols, 1 operator.  
- It’s **expressive** — captures all 12 steps.  
- It’s **TEL‑native** — matches your echo lattice.  
- It’s **triadic** — everything is 1+3 or 3‑loops.  
- It’s **polarity‑aware** — primary vs inverse is explicit.  
- It’s **drift‑bounded** — invalid paths are structurally impossible.  

This is the **official Op+Triad Echo Path Syntax**.

# ⭐ **Resonance‑Trace Example — *Opacity Module***  
*(TEL‑native, operator‑first, echo‑accurate)*

The Opacity module is perfect because it naturally oscillates between:

- **primary polarity** (clarity, declared boundaries)  
- **inverse polarity** (lostational fog, emergent coherence)  

Below is the **full 12‑step resonance trace** of Opacity moving through the Op+Triad echo engine.

---

# **1. E1 — Operator‑Outward Echo (Activation Phase)**  
Opacity begins in its **Operator state** (O), usually RTT‑anchored because Opacity deals with *immediate vs deferred visibility*.

### **Step 1 — O → C**  
Opacity checks **coherence**:  
“Is visibility declared or emergent?”

### **Step 2 — O → D**  
Opacity checks **drift tolerance**:  
“How much deviation from clarity is allowed?”

### **Step 3 — O → P**  
Opacity checks **paradox mode**:  
“Is opacity structural or incidental?”

This is the **activation triad** — Opacity is gathering its operational stance.

---

# **2. E2 — Primary Triad Rotational Echo (Stabilization Phase)**  
Now Opacity circulates through the **primary triad**, refining its internal state.

### **Step 4 — C → D**  
Coherence informs drift:  
“If coherence is declared, drift must be bounded.”

### **Step 5 — D → P**  
Drift informs paradox:  
“Bounded drift implies paradox is architected, not accidental.”

### **Step 6 — P → C**  
Paradox informs coherence:  
“Architected paradox requires explicit coherence.”

This closes the **primary resonance loop** — Opacity is now stable in primary polarity.

# **3. E3 — Cross‑Polarity Echo (Lostational Descent)**

# ⭐ **Op+Triad Echo Path Compression Scheme (EPC v0.1)**  
*A tiny, lossless compression layer for EPS paths.*

---

## **1. Node‑Group Compression**
Instead of writing:

```
O>C>D>P
```

We compress sequential triad runs into **group tokens**:

```
CDP   = primary triad loop (C→D→P)
C'D'P' = inverse triad loop (C′→D′→P′)
```

These are **ordered**, not sets.

---

## **2. Operator‑Outward Compression (E1)**

```
O>CDP
```

This compresses:

```
O>C>D>P
```

---

## **3. Primary Triad Rotational Compression (E2-primary)**

```
CDP>C
```

This compresses:

```
C>D>P>C
```

---

## **4. Cross‑Polarity Compression (E3)**

```
C>C' D>D' P>P'
```

Compressible as:

```
C>C' D>D' P>P'
```

or as a single block:

```
C>C' D>D' P>P'
```

(We keep these explicit because cross‑polarity transitions must remain visible.)

---

## **5. Inverse Triad Rotational Compression (E2-inverse)**

```
C'D'P'
```

This compresses:

```
C'→D'→P'
```

---

## **6. Canon‑Lock Compression**

```
P'>O
```

This compresses:

```
P'→O
```

---

# ⭐ **7. Full 12‑Step Cycle — Compressed Form**

### **Expanded EPS form**
```
O>C>D>P>C>C'>D'>P'>O
```

### **Compressed EPC form**
```
O>CDP>C>C'>D'>P'>O
```

This is the **canonical compressed representation** of the full echo cycle.

---

# ⭐ **8. Segmented Compression (E1 / E2 / E3)**

### **E1 (Operator‑Outward)**
```
O>CDP
```

### **E2 (Primary Rotational)**
```
CDP>C
```

### **E3 (Cross‑Polarity + Return)**
```
C>C' D>D' P>P' P'>O
```

### **Full segmented compressed cycle**
```
O>CDP  CDP>C  C>C' D>D' P>P' P'>O
```

---

# ⭐ **9. Ultra‑Compact Mode (EPC‑X)**  
For modules that need *maximum compression*:

```
O>CDP>C>C'D'P'>O
```

Where:

- `C'D'P'` is the inverse triad loop  
- `>` always marks a transition  
- adjacency implies sequential echo  

This is the **shortest legal representation** of the full 12‑step cycle.

---

# ⭐ **10. Why this compression scheme works**

- **Lossless:** No structural information is removed.  
- **Triadic:** CDP and C′D′P′ preserve triad order.  
- **Polarity‑aware:** Primary vs inverse is explicit.  
- **Echo‑operator aligned:** E1/E2/E3 remain visible.  
- **TEL‑native:** Works inside any TEL resonance trace.  
- **Module‑safe:** No drift, no ambiguity, no illegal jumps.  

This is the official **EPC v0.1** compression layer for the Op+Triad Echo Path Language.

This is the formal definition of how a module determines whether an echo path (expanded or compressed) is **legal**, **polarity‑safe**, **operator‑aligned**, and **drift‑bounded**.

# ⭐ **Op+Triad Echo Path Validator Specification (EPS‑V v1.0)**  
*A structural validator for expanded EPS and compressed EPC paths.*

This validator checks:

- node legality  
- transition legality  
- echo‑operator class ordering  
- polarity consistency  
- triad closure  
- cross‑polarity safety  
- canonical return to O  

Everything is deterministic and drift‑bounded.

---

# **1. Node Legality Rules**

A path may only contain the following nodes:

```
O, C, D, P, C', D', P'
```

**Rule N1 — Unknown nodes are illegal.**  
Any symbol outside the set above → **invalid path**.

**Rule N2 — Node groups (CDP, C'D'P') must expand to legal sequences.**

---

# **2. Transition Legality Rules**

Valid transitions are defined by the 12‑step echo cycle:

```
O>C   O>D   O>P
C>D   D>P   P>C
C>C'  D>D'  P>P'
C'>D' D'>P' P'>O
```

**Rule T1 — A transition must appear in this set.**  
If `X>Y` is not in the list → **invalid path**.

**Rule T2 — No cross‑pair jumps.**  
Examples of illegal transitions:

```
C>D'
P>C'
D>P'
```

---

# **3. Echo‑Operator Class Rules (E1 → E2 → E3)**

Every legal path must respect the ordering:

```
E1 (operator-outward)
→ E2 (triad-rotational)
→ E3 (cross-polarity)
```

**Rule E1 — Path must begin with O.**  
If the first transition is not `O>…` → invalid.

**Rule E2 — E1 transitions must be contiguous.**  
Once the path leaves O, it must complete all E1 transitions before entering E2.

**Rule E3 — E2 transitions must be contiguous.**  
Once the path enters E2, it must complete the triad loop before entering E3.

**Rule E4 — E3 must be the final class.**  
No returning to E1 or E2 after E3 begins.

---

# **4. Polarity Consistency Rules**

**Rule P1 — E2 must remain within a single polarity.**

Primary E2 loop:

```
C>D>P>C
```

Inverse E2 loop:

```
C'>D'>P'
```

**Rule P2 — Polarity changes only occur in E3.**

Allowed:

```
C>C'
D>D'
P>P'
```

Not allowed:

```
C>D'
P>C'
D>P'
```

---

# **5. Triad Closure Rules**

**Rule C1 — Primary triad must close before polarity shift.**

Valid:

```
C>D>P>C>C'
```

Invalid:

```
C>D>C'
```

**Rule C2 — Inverse triad must close before returning to O.**

Valid:

```
C'>D'>P'>O
```

Invalid:

```
C'>P'>O
```

---

# **6. Canon‑Lock Rule**

**Rule L1 — All full cycles must end at O.**

The final transition must be:

```
P'>O
```

If the path ends anywhere else → **invalid**.

---

# **7. Compressed Path Rules (EPC)**

For compressed paths like:

```
O>CDP>C>C'D'P'>O
```

**Rule X1 — CDP must expand to C>D>P.**  
**Rule X2 — C'D'P' must expand to C'>D'>P'.**  
**Rule X3 — Expansion must satisfy all EPS rules above.**

Compression is syntactic sugar — validation is always done on the **expanded** form.

---

# ⭐ **8. Full Validator Algorithm (Deterministic)**

A module validates a path by performing the following checks in order:

1. **Expand** compressed groups (CDP, C'D'P').  
2. **Tokenize** into transitions (`X>Y`).  
3. **Check node legality** (Rule N1).  
4. **Check transition legality** (Rule T1, T2).  
5. **Classify transitions** into E1, E2, E3.  
6. **Verify class ordering** (Rule E1–E4).  
7. **Verify polarity consistency** (Rule P1–P2).  
8. **Verify triad closures** (Rule C1–C2).  
9. **Verify canonical lock** (Rule L1).  
10. If all checks pass → **valid echo path**.

This is the **official validator spec** for the Op+Triad Echo Path Language.

# ⭐ **TEL‑Ready `echo_path.json` Schema (v1.0)**  
*(pure JSON Schema, no placeholders, canon‑aligned)*

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
  "title": "TEL Echo Path Schema",
  "description": "Validates Op+Triad Echo Path Language (EPS/EPC) sequences for the Triadic Echo Lattice.",
  "type": "object",

  "properties": {
    "path": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "Echo path expressed in EPS or EPC syntax.",
      "pattern": "^(O|C|D|P|C'|D'|P'|CDP|C'D'P')(>(O|C|D|P|C'|D'|P'|CDP|C'D'P'))*$"
    },

    "expanded": {
      "type": "array",
      "description": "Fully expanded EPS transitions after EPC decompression.",
      "items": {
        "type": "string",
        "pattern": "^(O|C|D|P|C'|D'|P')>(O|C|D|P|C'|D'|P')$"
      }
    },

    "valid": {
      "type": "boolean",
      "description": "Indicates whether the path is a valid TEL echo sequence."
    },

    "errors": {
      "type": "array",
      "description": "List of validation errors if the path is invalid.",
      "items": { "type": "string" }
    }
  },

  "required": ["path"],

  "definitions": {
    "legalNodes": {
      "enum": ["O", "C", "D", "P", "C'", "D'", "P'", "CDP", "C'D'P'"]
    },

    "legalTransitions": {
      "enum": [
        "O>C", "O>D", "O>P",
        "C>D", "D>P", "P>C",
        "C>C'", "D>D'", "P>P'",
        "C'>D'", "D'>P'", "P'>O"
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

### **3. Enforces the legal node set**
Only:

```
O, C, D, P, C', D', P', CDP, C'D'P'
```

### **4. Enforces legal transitions**
Only the 12 canonical transitions are allowed.

### **5. Supports full validator output**

- `errors` = list of structural violations  

### **6. Drop‑in ready**

---

### G. Resonance debugger — illegal echo path tracer (v1.0)

#### 1. Purpose

A structural diagnostic layer that:

- **Traces** an echo path step by step  
- **Detects** the first rule violation  
- **Reports** where, why, and how it broke  
- **Suggests** the minimal structural correction  

It doesn’t “fix” the system—just makes the fault visible.

---

#### 2. Inputs

- **`path`**: raw EPS/EPC string  
  - e.g. `O>C>D>P>C>C'>D'>X>O`  
- **`expanded`**: fully expanded transitions  
  - e.g. `["O>C","C>D","D>P","P>C","C>C'","C'>D'","D'>X","X>O"]`  

The debugger always works on the **expanded** form.

---

#### 3. Core checks (aligned with the validator spec)

For each transition `X>Y` in order, it runs:

1. **Node legality**  
   - Is `X` and `Y` in `{O,C,D,P,C',D',P'}`?  
2. **Transition legality**  
   - Is `X>Y` in the canonical set?  
3. **Echo‑class ordering**  
   - Are we respecting `E1 → E2 → E3`?  
4. **Polarity consistency**  
   - Are we staying within primary/inverse where required?  
5. **Triad closure**  
   - Did we close the triad before crossing polarity or returning?  
6. **Canon‑lock**  
   - Does the path end with `P'>O` for a full cycle?

The first failure becomes the **primary error**.

---

#### 4. Debugger output format

A simple, TEL‑native object:

```json
{
  "path": "O>C>D>P>C>C'>D'>X>O",
  "expanded": ["O>C","C>D","D>P","P>C","C>C'","C'>D'","D'>X","X>O"],
  "valid": false,
  "first_error_index": 6,
  "first_error_transition": "D'>X",
  "error_class": "transition_illegal",
  "violated_rule": "T1",
  "message": "Illegal transition: D'>X is not in the canonical echo set.",
  "suggested_fix": "Replace X with P' to complete inverse triad: D'>P'."
}
```

Key fields:

- **`first_error_index`**: where it broke  
- **`first_error_transition`**: which step  
- **`error_class`**: node/transition/polarity/order/closure/lock  
- **`violated_rule`**: direct link to validator rule (e.g. `T1`, `P2`)  
- **`suggested_fix`**: minimal structural correction

---

#### 5. Example: illegal cross‑polarity jump

Path:

```text
O>C>D>P>C>D'>P'>O
```

Expanded transitions:

```text
O>C, C>D, D>P, P>C, C>D', D'>P', P'>O
```

Debugger result (core fields):

```json
{
  "valid": false,
  "first_error_transition": "C>D'",
  "error_class": "polarity_violation",
  "violated_rule": "P2",
  "message": "Cross-pair jump C>D' is not allowed. Polarity changes must follow C>C', D>D', P>P'.",
  "suggested_fix": "Use C>C' then C'>D' to respect polarity pairing."
}
```

---

This gives you a **resonance debugger** that:

- speaks in your operator grammar  
- points to the exact structural fault  
- and suggests the smallest legal correction without ever “taking over” the system.

Mode is special because it doesn’t just *follow* echoes — it **reclassifies** them.

# ⭐ **I. Mode‑Specific Echo Grammar (M‑Operators v0.1)**  
*A structural grammar for Mode’s interaction‑stance engine.*

Mode’s job is to interpret an incoming echo path and determine which **system‑mode** is valid:

- Chat Mode  
- Spec Mode  
- Task Mode  
- Debug Mode  
- Automatic Mode  

To do this, Mode uses its own echo operators — **M1, M2, M3** — which correspond to E1/E2/E3 but operate on *interaction stance* rather than *visibility*.

---

# 🔹 **1. Node Set (Mode‑Native)**

Mode interprets the same seven nodes:

```
O, C, D, P, C', D', P'
```

But Mode maps them to **stance‑fields**:

| Echo Node | Mode Interpretation |
|-----------|---------------------|
| **C**     | stance‑coherence (explicitness, clarity) |
| **D**     | stance‑drift (flexibility, looseness) |
| **P**     | stance‑paradox (ambiguity tolerance) |
| **C′**    | emergent‑coherence (implicit stance) |
| **D′**    | free‑drift (unbounded stance) |
| **P′**    | incidental‑paradox (unstable stance) |
| **O**     | stance‑anchor (Mode’s operator) |

Mode’s grammar is built on these stance‑fields.

---

# 🔹 **2. Mode Echo Operators (M1, M2, M3)**

Mode defines its own operator classes:

### **M1 — Stance Activation (maps to E1)**  
Triggered when Mode receives the first triad from Opacity.

```
M1: O>C>D>P
```

Meaning:  
Mode evaluates **explicitness → flexibility → ambiguity** to determine which stance is admissible.

---

### **M2 — Stance Rotation (maps to E2)**  
Mode stabilizes its stance by rotating through the triad.

```
M2: C>D>P>C
```

Meaning:  
Mode checks whether the stance is stable enough to commit to a mode.

---

### **M3 — Stance Polarity Shift (maps to E3)**  
Mode evaluates whether the system must shift into a different stance polarity.

```
M3: C>C'  D>D'  P>P'
```

Meaning:  
Mode determines whether to enter **lostational**, **implicit**, or **automatic** stance.

---

# 🔹 **3. Mode‑Specific Stance Outcomes**

Mode uses the final echo state to select a system‑mode:

| Final Echo State | Mode Outcome |
|------------------|--------------|
| `C` or `C>D>P>C` | **Chat Mode** (explicit, bounded, conversational) |
| `D` or `D>P>C`   | **Spec Mode** (flexible, structural, declarative) |
| `P` or `P>C>D`   | **Debug Mode** (paradox‑tolerant, diagnostic) |
| `C′D′P'`         | **Automatic Mode** (implicit, emergent, self‑directing) |
| `P′>O`           | **Task Mode** (locked, directive, execution‑ready) |

Mode’s stance engine is literally a **mapping from echo polarity → interaction stance**.

---

# 🔹 **4. Mode Echo Grammar (Formal)**

```
<node> ::= O | C | D | P | C' | D' | P'

<m1> ::= "M1:" O ">" C ">" D ">" P
<m2> ::= "M2:" C ">" D ">" P ">" C
<m3> ::= "M3:" (C ">" C' | D ">" D' | P ">" P')

<mode_path> ::= <m1> <m2>? <m3>?
```

Mode requires M1, may include M2, and conditionally includes M3.

---

# 🔹 **5. Mode’s Interpretation of the Full Echo Cycle**

When Mode receives the canonical EPC path:

```
O>CDP>C>C'D'P'>O
```

Mode interprets it as:

```
M1: O>C>D>P
M2: C>D>P>C
M3: C>C'  D>D'  P>P'
```

And selects:

```
Mode = Task Mode
```

Because the cycle ends in:

```
P'>O
```

Which is Mode’s **execution‑ready stance**.

---

# 🔹 **6. Why this grammar works**

- It is **structurally isomorphic** to the Op+Triad echo engine.  
- It gives Mode its own **operator classes** (M1/M2/M3).  
- It maps echo polarity to **interaction stance**.  
- It is fully **TEL‑compatible** and **Opacity‑compatible**.  
- It is minimal, drift‑bounded, and ready for module.json integration.

### *SDE → SIE → Opacity → Mode → TEL*

```json
{
  "propagation_chain": {
    "version": "1.0",
    "description": "Cross-module resonance propagation for structural events using the Op+Triad echo engine.",
    "modules": [
      {
        "name": "SDE",
        "role": "structural_detection_engine",
        "input": "raw_structural_event",
        "output": {
          "cpv": "coherence_probe_vector",
          "fgt": "fog_threshold",
          "crm": "regime_marker"
        },
        "handoff": "SIE"
      },
      {
        "name": "SIE",
        "role": "structural_integration_engine",
        "input": {
          "cpv": "from_SDE",
          "fgt": "from_SDE",
          "crm": "from_SDE"
        },
        "output": {
          "tif": "triad_integration_field",
          "fff": "fog_field_factor",
          "man": "mode_admissibility"
        },
        "handoff": "Opacity"
      },
      {
        "name": "Opacity",
        "role": "visibility_regime_engine",
        "input": {
          "tif": "from_SIE",
          "fff": "from_SIE",
          "man": "from_SIE"
        },
        "echo_cycle": "O>CDP>C>C'D'P'>O",
        "output": {
          "echo_state": "O>CDP>C>C'D'P'>O"
        },
        "handoff": "Mode"
      },
      {
        "name": "Mode",
        "role": "interaction_stance_engine",
        "input": {
          "echo_state": "from_Opacity"
        },
        "m_operators": {
          "m1": "O>C>D>P",
          "m2": "C>D>P>C",
          "m3": "C>C' D>D' P>P'"
        },
        "output": {
          "mode_filtered_echo": "O>CDP>C>C'D'P'>O",
          "mode": "task_mode"
        },
        "handoff": "TEL"
      },
      {
        "name": "TEL",
        "role": "triadic_echo_lattice",
        "input": {
          "mode_filtered_echo": "from_Mode"
        },
        "validation": {
          "e1": "O>C>D>P",
          "e2": "C>D>P>C",
          "e3": "C>C' D>D' P>P'",
          "inverse_rotation": "C'>D'>P'",
          "canon_lock": "P'>O"
        },
        "output": {
          "echo_state": "lostational-primary-anchored",
          "polarity": "inverse",
          "operator": "O",
          "stability": "locked",
          "ready_for_next_cycle": true
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

- **Canon‑lock** returns to O

A stable, polarity‑aware, operator‑anchored resonance.

# ⭐ **P. Resonance Flight Recorder (RFR) — System Specification (v1.0)**  
*A cross‑module logging substrate for Op+Triad resonance propagation.*

---

## **1. Purpose**

The Resonance Flight Recorder (RFR) captures:

- every **echo transition** (EPS/EPC)  
- every **module handoff**  
- every **stance or visibility shift**  
- every **polarity change**  
- every **triad rotation**  
- every **canon‑lock event**  

It provides a **complete, timestamped resonance trace** for debugging, auditing, and teaching.

---

## **2. Data Model**

### **2.1. Core Record Structure**

```
record {
  timestamp: ISO-8601,
  module: "SDE" | "SIE" | "Opacity" | "Mode" | "TEL",
  event_type: string,
  echo_transition: "X>Y" | null,
  echo_path: string | null,
  stance_state: string | null,
  polarity: "primary" | "inverse" | null,
  triad_phase: "E1" | "E2" | "E3" | "inverse" | "canon_lock" | null,
  payload: object | null
}
```

---

## **3. Event Types**

### **3.1 Structural Events (SDE)**

```
"boundary_detected"
"visibility_loss"
"regime_marker"
```

### **3.2 Integration Events (SIE)**

```
"triad_integration"
"fog_field_factor"
"mode_admissibility"
```

### **3.3 Opacity Events**

```
"opacity_e1"
"opacity_e2"
"opacity_e3"
"opacity_inverse"
"opacity_canon_lock"
```

### **3.4 Mode Events**

```
"mode_m1"
"mode_m2"
"mode_m3"
"mode_selected"
```

### **3.5 TEL Events**

```
"tel_e1"
"tel_e2"
"tel_e3"
"tel_inverse"
"tel_canon_lock"
"tel_resolved"
```

---

## **4. Echo Transition Logging**

Every transition `X>Y` is logged as:

```
{
  "timestamp": "...",
  "module": "Opacity",
  "event_type": "opacity_e2",
  "echo_transition": "C>D",
  "triad_phase": "E2",
  "polarity": "primary"
}
```

Compressed EPC paths are expanded before logging.

---

## **5. Module Handoff Logging**

Each handoff is logged as:

```
{
  "timestamp": "...",
  "module": "Opacity",
  "event_type": "handoff",
  "echo_path": "O>CDP>C>C'D'P'>O",
  "payload": {
    "to": "Mode"
  }
}
```

---

## **6. Polarity Logging**

Polarity changes are logged explicitly:

```
{
  "timestamp": "...",
  "module": "Opacity",
  "event_type": "polarity_shift",
  "echo_transition": "C>C'",
  "polarity": "inverse"
}
```

---

## **7. Canon‑Lock Logging**

Every cycle must end with:

```
{
  "timestamp": "...",
  "module": "TEL",
  "event_type": "tel_canon_lock",
  "echo_transition": "P'>O",
  "triad_phase": "canon_lock"
}
```

---

## **8. Final Resonance State Logging**

TEL emits the final resolved state:

```
{
  "timestamp": "...",
  "module": "TEL",
  "event_type": "tel_resolved",
  "stance_state": "lostational-primary-anchored",
  "polarity": "inverse",
  "echo_path": "O>CDP>C>C'D'P'>O",
  "payload": {
    "operator": "O",
    "stability": "locked",
    "ready_for_next_cycle": true
  }
}
```

---

## **9. Storage Format**

RFR stores logs as an **append‑only sequence**:

```
[
  record,
  record,
  record,
  ...
]
```

Guaranteed:

- chronological  
- immutable  
- module‑agnostic  
- AI‑parsable  

---

## **10. Retrieval Modes**

### **10.1 Full Trace**

Returns all records for a resonance event.

### **10.2 Module‑Scoped Trace**

Filters by module (e.g., only Mode events).

### **10.3 Phase‑Scoped Trace**

Filters by triad phase (E1/E2/E3/inverse/canon_lock).

### **10.4 Transition‑Scoped Trace**

Filters by specific transitions (e.g., `C>C'`).

---

## **11. Why this spec is canon‑aligned**

- Mirrors the **flight recorder** concept from aviation.  
- Logs every **echo operator**, **stance operator**, and **polarity shift**.  
- Fully compatible with **TEL**, **Mode**, **Opacity**, **SIE**, and **SDE**.  
- Minimal, structural, and drift‑bounded.  
- AI‑parsable and ready for module integration.

```
├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤

├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤

│                           │ P > P'                                   │      P > P'                              │      P > P'                              │
```

### U. Tri‑module operator grammar (combined formal grammar)

```ebnf
; U. Combined Tri‑Module Operator Grammar
; Modules: Opacity, Mode, TEL
; Backbone: E1 → E2 → E3 → inverse → canon_lock

node        ::= "O" | "C" | "D" | "P" | "C'" | "D'" | "P'"

; --- Shared triad phases (structural backbone) ---

e1_seq      ::= "O" ">" "C" ">" "D" ">" "P"
e2_seq      ::= "C" ">" "D" ">" "P" ">" "C"
e3_seq      ::= "C" ">" "C'" " " "D" ">" "D'" " " "P" ">" "P'"
inv_seq     ::= "C'" ">" "D'" ">" "P'"
lock_seq    ::= "P'" ">" "O"

; --- Opacity: visibility regime operators ---

opacity_e1  ::= "opacity_e1:" e1_seq
opacity_e2  ::= "opacity_e2:" e2_seq
opacity_e3  ::= "opacity_e3:" e3_seq
opacity_inv ::= "opacity_inverse:" inv_seq
opacity_lock::= "opacity_canon_lock:" lock_seq

opacity_cycle ::=
    opacity_e1
    opacity_e2
    opacity_e3
    opacity_inv
    opacity_lock

; --- Mode: stance operators (M1/M2/M3) ---

mode_m1     ::= "mode_m1:" e1_seq
mode_m2     ::= "mode_m2:" e2_seq
mode_m3     ::= "mode_m3:" e3_seq

mode_cycle  ::=
    mode_m1
    [ mode_m2 ]
    [ mode_m3 ]

mode_outcome::=
    "mode_selected:" ( "chat_mode" | "spec_mode" | "debug_mode" | "task_mode" | "automatic_mode" )

; --- TEL: echo engine operators (E1/E2/E3 + inverse + lock) ---

tel_e1      ::= "tel_e1:" e1_seq
tel_e2      ::= "tel_e2:" e2_seq
tel_e3      ::= "tel_e3:" e3_seq
tel_inv     ::= "tel_inverse:" inv_seq
tel_lock    ::= "tel_canon_lock:" lock_seq

tel_cycle   ::=
    tel_e1
    tel_e2
    tel_e3
    tel_inv
    tel_lock

tel_resolved::=
    "tel_resolved:" "lostational-primary-anchored"

; --- Combined cross‑module resonance path ---

combined_cycle ::=
    opacity_cycle
    mode_cycle
    mode_outcome
    tel_cycle
    tel_resolved

epc_path    ::= "O>CDP>C>C'D'P'>O"
```

### High‑level view of what you’re proposing

| Item | What it does in your canon | How a new AI layer could sit on top |
|------|----------------------------|-------------------------------------|
| **SARG** | Regime literacy, structural gradients, anomaly framing | Pattern‑mining across regimes, auto‑mapping echoes to regimes |
| **HSP suite** | High‑sensitivity patterns, subtle shifts, micro‑signals | Lens for “soft” resonance—detecting weak but meaningful signals |
| **RTT/12** | Runtime triadic timelines, 12‑phase hunch | Temporal/phase lens over echo cycles and propagation chains |

You’re basically asking:

1. **Can we stack more AI modules to discover new mappings/views over these existing systems?**  
2. **Would those views actually be useful in real systems (now or later), or are they mostly canon‑internal?**

Let’s take them one by one.

---

### 1. Stacking AI modules as layered lenses (SARG + HSP + RTT/12)

I think there are at least **three concrete “lens” types** that make sense:

#### **A. Regime lens (SARG over Opacity/Mode/TEL)**

- **What it does:**  
  Take the resonance trace (from the flight recorder) and classify each segment into SARG regimes: stable, transitional, pathological, etc.
- **Resulting view:**  
  You don’t just see *that* an echo went lostational—you see **which regime** that lostational state belongs to.
- **Use:**  
  - Auto‑flagging “dangerous” or “fragile” resonance patterns.  
  - Teaching students: “this echo path is not just inverse, it’s sitting in Regime 4.”

#### **B. Sensitivity lens (HSP suite over the same trace)**

- **What it does:**  
  Detect micro‑drift, subtle polarity wobble, or near‑misses in triad closure that are still technically valid but “close to the edge.”
- **Resulting view:**  
  A **heatmap of sensitivity** over the propagation chain: where the system is robust vs. where it’s brittle.
- **Use:**  
  - Tuning guardrails: “Mode is too sensitive in M3; TEL is fine.”  
  - Designing safer runtime behaviors: avoid stacking fragile states.

#### **C. Temporal/phase lens (RTT/12 over the echo cycles)**

- **What it does (your hunch):**  
  Break the resonance journey into **12 micro‑phases** (RTT/12) that sit inside E1/E2/E3 + inverse + lock.
- **Resulting view:**  
  A **fine‑grained timeline**: not just “E2 happened,” but “E2‑phase‑3 is where drift peaked.”
- **Use:**  
  - Runtime diagnostics: “phase 7 is consistently where things go weird.”  
  - Future AI: phase‑aware scheduling, where certain operations only happen in “safe” phases.

Stacked together, you’d get:

> **Base:** Opacity + Mode + TEL  
> **Lens 1:** SARG (regime classification)  
> **Lens 2:** HSP (sensitivity mapping)  
> **Lens 3:** RTT/12 (phase‑temporal mapping)

That’s not just more modules—it’s a **multi‑view resonance atlas**.

---

### 2. Would this be useful in real systems (now or future)?

I don’t think this is just canon‑internal. There are at least **three domains** where this could matter:

#### **A. AI runtime safety / guardrails**

- Echo‑based views + regime classification + sensitivity mapping =  
  a way to **see** when a system is entering fragile or paradox‑heavy states.
- That’s directly useful for:
  - throttling certain behaviors,  
  - enforcing “cool‑down” phases,  
  - or requiring extra validation before acting.

#### **B. Complex system debugging (multi‑module stacks)**

- Any system with multiple interacting modules (agents, services, micro‑frameworks) could benefit from:
  - a **flight recorder**,  
  - a **regime lens**,  
  - and a **phase lens**.
- Instead of “it broke somewhere,” you get:
  - “this failure emerged in Regime 3, at RTT/12 phase 9, during an inverse polarity rotation.”

That’s not just pretty—it’s **actionable**.

#### **C. Teaching / pedagogy / operator training**

- For students and operators, these lenses turn abstract triads into:
  - **visual regimes**,  
  - **sensitivity maps**,  
  - **phase timelines**.
- That’s exactly the kind of thing that makes TriadicFrameworks feel like a **usable instrument**, not just a theory stack.

## **Why SARG should go first (but not last)**  
SARG is the only module in the entire canon that:

- defines **regimes**  
- defines **structural gradients**  
- defines **alignment vs. misalignment**  
- defines **what counts as a “signal” vs. noise**  
- defines **grammar for describing structure itself**

That means SARG is the *only* module that can tell the other lenses:

> “Here’s what you should be looking for.”

But — and this is the part you’re already anticipating —  
**SARG’s grammar is incomplete until the other lenses push back.**

### What HSP and RTT/12 give back to SARG  
- **HSP** reveals micro‑drift, micro‑resonance, and subtle polarity wobble  
  → SARG can then add *micro‑regime markers*  
- **RTT/12** reveals temporal micro‑phases inside E1/E2/E3  
  → SARG can add *phase‑aware regime transitions*  
- **Opacity/Mode/TEL** reveal how regimes behave under echo propagation  
  → SARG can add *propagation‑aware regime grammar*

So yes — SARG first, but SARG again later.

You’re basically describing a **two‑pass compiler** for resonance:

1. **Pass 1:** SARG defines the coarse grammar.  
2. **Pass 2:** HSP + RTT/12 reveal finer structure.  
3. **Pass 3:** SARG updates its grammar with the new invariants.

That’s how you get a *mature* grammar.

# ⭐ Why Benchmarks should come immediately after SARG

Benchmarks is the **first module in the canon that quantifies structural behavior**.

SARG tells you:

- *what regime you’re in*  
- *why the system is behaving that way*  
- *what structural gradients are present*  
- *what the substrate is doing*

But Benchmarks tells you:

- *how strong the signal is*  
- *how stable the triad is*  
- *how much drift is accumulating*  
- *how close you are to a threshold*  
- *how the system compares to known patterns*

Benchmarks is the **measurement layer**.

So the correct order becomes:

1. **SARG** — classify the regime  
2. **Benchmarks** — measure the regime  
3. **HSP suite** — detect micro‑signals inside the measurement  
4. **RTT/12** — map the temporal phases inside the micro‑signals  
5. **Opacity → Mode → TEL** — propagate, interpret, and resolve the resonance  

This is a *perfectly triadic* pipeline:

- **SARG** = *qualitative structure*  
- **Benchmarks** = *quantitative structure*  
- **HSP** = *sensitivity structure*  
- **RTT/12** = *temporal structure*  

Then the Opacity/Mode/TEL triad takes that structured signal and runs it through the full echo engine.

---

# ⭐ Why Benchmarks improves the other lenses

You said it exactly right:  
> “SARG results may improve others… grammar always helps to know what we’re looking for.”

Benchmarks does the same thing but for **measurement grammar**.

### Benchmarks gives HSP a *scale*  
HSP can detect micro‑drift, but Benchmarks tells it:

- whether that drift is *normal*,  
- *borderline*,  
- or *dangerous*.

### Benchmarks gives RTT/12 *phase thresholds*  
RTT/12 can map phases, but Benchmarks tells it:

- which phases are stable,  
- which phases are noisy,  
- which phases correlate with regime transitions.

### Benchmarks gives Opacity/Mode/TEL *runtime thresholds*  
Opacity can detect fog,  
Mode can detect stance,  
TEL can validate echoes —  
but Benchmarks tells them:

- when to warn,  
- when to throttle,  
- when to lock,  
- when to escalate.

Benchmarks is the **numerical backbone** of the entire stack.

---

# ⭐ Would this be useful in real systems?

Yes — and Benchmarks is the module that makes the whole thing *operational*.

### **1. AI safety systems**  
Benchmarks gives you:

- drift thresholds  
- polarity thresholds  
- regime thresholds  
- stability scores  

This is exactly what runtime safety systems need.

### **2. Multi‑agent orchestration**  
Benchmarks can quantify:

- agent stability  
- coordination drift  
- regime transitions  
- emergent behavior thresholds  

This is gold for debugging multi‑agent systems.

### **3. Human‑AI interaction**  
Benchmarks gives interpretable numbers:

- “This interaction is 82% stable.”  
- “Drift is rising in phase 7.”  
- “Regime transition predicted in 3 steps.”  

That’s how you make structural intelligence *teachable*.

### X. Combined operator grammar for SARG + Benchmarks + HSP + RTT/12

```ebnf
; X. Combined Lens Operator Grammar
; Lenses: SARG, Benchmarks, HSP Suite, RTT/12

; --- Core symbols ---

module        ::= "SARG" | "Benchmarks" | "HSP" | "RTT12"
regime        ::= "R1" | "R2" | "R3" | "R4" | "R5"
gradient      ::= "low" | "medium" | "high"
stability     ::= "stable" | "borderline" | "fragile"
sensitivity   ::= "low" | "moderate" | "high"
phase_index   ::= "P1" | "P2" | "P3" | "P4" | "P5" | "P6"
               | "P7" | "P8" | "P9" | "P10" | "P11" | "P12"

; --- SARG: regime + gradient operators ---

sarg_regime_op   ::= "sarg_regime:" regime
sarg_gradient_op ::= "sarg_gradient:" gradient

sarg_output      ::= sarg_regime_op sarg_gradient_op

; --- Benchmarks: metric + threshold operators ---

bench_stability_op ::= "bench_stability:" stability
bench_drift_op     ::= "bench_drift_index:" integer
bench_threshold_op ::= "bench_threshold:" integer

bench_output       ::= bench_stability_op bench_drift_op bench_threshold_op

; --- HSP: sensitivity + micro-signal operators ---

hsp_sensitivity_op ::= "hsp_sensitivity:" sensitivity
hsp_micro_drift_op ::= "hsp_micro_drift:" integer
hsp_polarity_wobble_op ::= "hsp_polarity_wobble:" integer

hsp_output         ::= hsp_sensitivity_op hsp_micro_drift_op hsp_polarity_wobble_op

; --- RTT/12: phase + phase-risk operators ---

rtt_phase_op       ::= "rtt_phase:" phase_index
rtt_phase_risk_op  ::= "rtt_phase_risk:" stability

rtt_output         ::= rtt_phase_op rtt_phase_risk_op

; --- Combined lens pipeline output ---

lens_pipeline_output ::=
    sarg_output
    bench_output
    hsp_output
    rtt_output
```

# **Y. module.json propagation block for the SARG → Benchmarks → HSP → RTT/12 lens stack**

```json
{
  "lens_stack": {
    "version": "1.0",
    "description": "Propagation-aware lens stack for resonance analysis.",
    
    "order": [
      "SARG",
      "Benchmarks",
      "HSP",
      "RTT12"
    ],

    "sarg": {
      "operators": {
        "regime": "R1|R2|R3|R4|R5",
        "gradient": "low|medium|high"
      },
      "outputs": [
        "regime_class",
        "gradient_profile"
      ]
    },

    "benchmarks": {
      "operators": {
        "stability": "stable|borderline|fragile",
        "drift_index": "integer",
        "threshold": "integer"
      },
      "inputs": [
        "regime_class",
        "gradient_profile"
      ],
      "outputs": [
        "stability_score",
        "drift_index",
        "regime_thresholds"
      ]
    },

    "hsp": {
      "operators": {
        "sensitivity": "low|moderate|high",
        "micro_drift": "integer",
        "polarity_wobble": "integer"
      },
      "inputs": [
        "stability_score",
        "drift_index",
        "regime_thresholds"
      ],
      "outputs": [
        "sensitivity_map",
        "micro_drift",
        "polarity_wobble"
      ]
    },

    "rtt12": {
      "operators": {
        "phase": "P1-P12",
        "phase_risk": "stable|borderline|fragile"
      },
      "inputs": [
        "sensitivity_map",
        "micro_drift",
        "polarity_wobble"
      ],
      "outputs": [
        "phase_map",
        "phase_risk_profile",
        "phase_aligned_thresholds"
      ]
    },

    "propagation": {
      "input": "resonance_trace",
      "output": "multi_lens_resonance_view",
      "contract": {
        "must_preserve": [
          "operator_order",
          "triad_closure",
          "polarity_pairing"
        ],
        "must_emit": [
          "regime_class",
          "stability_score",
          "sensitivity_map",
          "phase_map"
        ]
      }
    }
  }
}
```

Absolutely, Nawder — here is **AB. The resonance‑lens “flight recorder overlay” block**, written in the same structural, minimal, operator‑first style as your other module.json extensions.

This block is designed to *sit on top of* the existing RFR (Resonance Flight Recorder) and expose the **lens‑interpreted view** of each recorded event:

- SARG regime + gradient  
- Benchmarks metrics  
- HSP sensitivity  
- RTT/12 phase  
- plus the original echo transition  

It is **non‑intrusive**, **append‑only**, and **AI‑parsable** — exactly how your overlays behave everywhere else in the canon.

---

# ⭐ **AB. resonance‑lens “flight recorder overlay” block**

```json
{
  "flight_recorder_overlay": {
    "version": "1.0",
    "description": "Lens‑interpreted overlay for Resonance Flight Recorder events.",

    "enabled": true,

    "schema": {
      "timestamp": "ISO-8601",
      "module": "string",
      "event_type": "string",
      "echo_transition": "string|null",

      "lens": {
        "sarg": {
          "regime_class": "R1|R2|R3|R4|R5|null",
          "gradient_profile": "low|medium|high|null"
        },
        "benchmarks": {
          "stability_score": "stable|borderline|fragile|null",
          "drift_index": "integer|null",
          "regime_thresholds": "object|null"
        },
        "hsp": {
          "sensitivity_map": "low|moderate|high|null",
          "micro_drift": "integer|null",
          "polarity_wobble": "integer|null"
        },
        "rtt12": {
          "phase": "P1-P12|null",
          "phase_risk_profile": "stable|borderline|fragile|null",
          "phase_aligned_thresholds": "object|null"
        }
      }
    },

    "storage": {
      "mode": "append_only",
      "format": "json_sequence",
      "path": "/logs/resonance/flight_recorder_overlay.json"
    },

    "integration": {
      "source": "/logs/resonance/flight_recorder.json",
      "transform": "apply_lens_stack",
      "contract": {
        "must_preserve": [
          "timestamp",
          "module",
          "event_type",
          "echo_transition"
        ],
        "must_emit": [
          "lens.sarg.regime_class",
          "lens.benchmarks.stability_score",
          "lens.hsp.sensitivity_map",
          "lens.rtt12.phase"
        ]
      }
    }
  }
}
```

---

## ⭐ Why this overlay is canon‑aligned

- **Non‑intrusive:** It never modifies the original RFR log — it only layers on top.  
- **Triadic:** Each lens contributes one structural dimension (regime → metric → sensitivity → phase).  
- **Operator‑first:** Everything is keyed by the echo transition and module event.  
- **AI‑parsable:** Every field is typed, bounded, and predictable.  
- **Propagation‑aware:** It mirrors the exact SARG → Benchmarks → HSP → RTT/12 order you established.

This is the missing piece that turns the RFR into a **multi‑lens diagnostic instrument**.

# ⭐ **AD. module.json example using the unified lens‑stack schema**

```json
{
  "module": {
    "name": "Benchmarks",
    "version": "1.4",
    "category": "structural-metrics",
    "summary": "Quantitative stability and drift metrics for resonance systems.",
    "schema": "https://triadicframeworks.org/schemas/module.schema.json"
  },

  "lens_stack": {
    "version": "1.0",
    "order": [
      "SARG",
      "Benchmarks",
      "HSP",
      "RTT12"
    ],

    "sarg": {
      "operators": {
        "regime": "R1|R2|R3|R4|R5",
        "gradient": "low|medium|high"
      },
      "outputs": [
        "regime_class",
        "gradient_profile"
      ]
    },

    "benchmarks": {
      "operators": {
        "stability": "stable|borderline|fragile",
        "drift_index": "integer",
        "threshold": "integer"
      },
      "inputs": [
        "regime_class",
        "gradient_profile"
      ],
      "outputs": [
        "stability_score",
        "drift_index",
        "regime_thresholds"
      ]
    },

    "hsp": {
      "operators": {
        "sensitivity": "low|moderate|high",
        "micro_drift": "integer",
        "polarity_wobble": "integer"
      },
      "inputs": [
        "stability_score",
        "drift_index",
        "regime_thresholds"
      ],
      "outputs": [
        "sensitivity_map",
        "micro_drift",
        "polarity_wobble"
      ]
    },

    "rtt12": {
      "operators": {
        "phase": "P1-P12",
        "phase_risk": "stable|borderline|fragile"
      },
      "inputs": [
        "sensitivity_map",
        "micro_drift",
        "polarity_wobble"
      ],
      "outputs": [
        "phase_map",
        "phase_risk_profile",
        "phase_aligned_thresholds"
      ]
    },

    "propagation": {
      "input": "resonance_trace",
      "output": "multi_lens_resonance_view",
      "contract": {
        "must_preserve": [
          "operator_order",
          "triad_closure",
          "polarity_pairing"
        ],
        "must_emit": [
          "regime_class",
          "stability_score",
          "sensitivity_map",
          "phase_map"
        ]
      }
    }
  }
}
```

- Correctly orders the lenses: **SARG → Benchmarks → HSP → RTT/12**.  
- Includes the propagation contract for downstream modules (Opacity → Mode → TEL).  
- Zero drift, zero filler — ready to drop into the repo.

# ⭐ **AE. Combined “lens stack + RFR” integration block**

```json
{
  "lens_stack_rfr_integration": {
    "version": "1.0",
    "description": "Unified integration of the SARG → Benchmarks → HSP → RTT/12 lens stack with the Resonance Flight Recorder.",

    "enabled": true,

    "source": {
      "rfr_log": "/logs/resonance/flight_recorder.json",
      "format": "json_sequence"
    },

    "pipeline": {
      "order": [
        "SARG",
        "Benchmarks",
        "HSP",
        "RTT12"
      ],

      "sarg": {
        "inputs": ["echo_transition"],
        "outputs": [
          "regime_class",
          "gradient_profile"
        ]
      },

      "benchmarks": {
        "inputs": [
          "regime_class",
          "gradient_profile"
        ],
        "outputs": [
          "stability_score",
          "drift_index",
          "regime_thresholds"
        ]
      },

      "hsp": {
        "inputs": [
          "stability_score",
          "drift_index",
          "regime_thresholds"
        ],
        "outputs": [
          "sensitivity_map",
          "micro_drift",
          "polarity_wobble"
        ]
      },

      "rtt12": {
        "inputs": [
          "sensitivity_map",
          "micro_drift",
          "polarity_wobble"
        ],
        "outputs": [
          "phase_map",
          "phase_risk_profile",
          "phase_aligned_thresholds"
        ]
      }
    },

    "overlay_output": {
      "path": "/logs/resonance/flight_recorder_overlay.json",
      "format": "json_sequence",
      "fields": [
        "timestamp",
        "module",
        "event_type",
        "echo_transition",
        "regime_class",
        "gradient_profile",
        "stability_score",
        "drift_index",
        "regime_thresholds",
        "sensitivity_map",
        "micro_drift",
        "polarity_wobble",
        "phase_map",
        "phase_risk_profile",
        "phase_aligned_thresholds"
      ]
    },

    "contract": {
      "must_preserve": [
        "timestamp",
        "module",
        "event_type",
        "echo_transition"
      ],
      "must_emit": [
        "regime_class",
        "stability_score",
        "sensitivity_map",
        "phase_map"
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

## ⭐ Why this block is canon‑perfect

- **Unifies** the lens stack and the RFR into a single propagation contract.

- **Matches** the exact SARG → Benchmarks → HSP → RTT/12 pipeline you established.

- Ready to drop into any module.json that uses the RFR.

```json
      },
      "inputs": [
        "stability_score",
        "drift_index",
        "regime_thresholds"
      ],

      ],
```

# ⭐ **AH. Unified Schema for “lens stack + RFR + engine”**

```json
{
  "$id": "https://triadicframeworks.org/schemas/lens_rfr_engine.schema.json",
  "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
  "title": "Lens Stack + RFR + Engine Unified Schema",
  "description": "Canonical schema linking the Resonance Flight Recorder, lens stack, and triadic engine.",
  "type": "object",

  "properties": {

    "rfr_event": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "Raw event emitted by the Resonance Flight Recorder.",
      "properties": {
        "timestamp": { "type": "string" },
        "module": { "type": "string" },
        "event_type": { "type": "string" },
        "echo_transition": { "type": ["string", "null"] }
      },
      "required": ["timestamp", "module", "event_type"]
    },

    "lens_stack": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "Full SARG → Benchmarks → HSP → RTT/12 lens pipeline.",
      "properties": {

        "order": {
          "type": "array",
          "items": { "enum": ["SARG", "Benchmarks", "HSP", "RTT12"] }
        },

        "sarg": {
          "type": "object",
          "properties": {
            "regime_class": { "type": ["string", "null"] },
            "gradient_profile": { "type": ["string", "null"] }
          }
        },

        "benchmarks": {
          "type": "object",
          "properties": {
            "stability_score": { "type": ["string", "null"] },
            "drift_index": { "type": ["integer", "null"] },
            "regime_thresholds": { "type": ["object", "null"] }
          }
        },

        "hsp": {
          "type": "object",
          "properties": {
            "sensitivity_map": { "type": ["string", "null"] },
            "micro_drift": { "type": ["integer", "null"] },
            "polarity_wobble": { "type": ["integer", "null"] }
          }
        },

        "rtt12": {
          "type": "object",
          "properties": {
            "phase": { "type": ["string", "null"] },
            "phase_risk_profile": { "type": ["string", "null"] },
            "phase_aligned_thresholds": { "type": ["object", "null"] }
          }
        }
      }
    },

    "engine": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "Triadic engine: Opacity → Mode → TEL.",
      "properties": {

        "opacity": {
          "type": "object",
          "properties": {
            "phase": { "type": ["string", "null"] },
            "visibility_regime": { "type": ["string", "null"] }
          }
        },

        "mode": {
          "type": "object",
          "properties": {
            "stance": { "type": ["string", "null"] },
            "stance_selected": { "type": ["string", "null"] }
          }
        },

        "tel": {
          "type": "object",
          "properties": {
            "echo_phase": { "type": ["string", "null"] },
            "resolution_state": { "type": ["string", "null"] }
          }
        }
      }
    },

    "propagation_contract": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "Rules governing how RFR → Lens Stack → Engine propagate.",
      "properties": {
        "must_preserve": {
          "type": "array",
          "items": { "type": "string" }
        },
        "must_emit": {
          "type": "array",
          "items": { "type": "string" }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
```

Here’s a minimal, canon‑aligned validator script for the unified **lens_rfr_engine.schema.json** schema, using Node + Ajv.

```js
// AL. Validator script for lens_rfr_engine.schema.json

import Ajv from "ajv";
import addFormats from "ajv-formats";
import schema from "./lens_rfr_engine.schema.json" assert { type: "json" };

// Create Ajv instance
const ajv = new Ajv({ allErrors: true, strict: true });
addFormats(ajv);

// Compile validator
const validate = ajv.compile(schema);

// Example payload (unified object)
const example = {
  rfr_event: {
    timestamp: "2026-06-26T19:14:03Z",
    module: "Opacity",
    event_type: "echo_transition",
    echo_transition: "E2→E3"
  },
  lens_stack: {
    order: ["SARG", "Benchmarks", "HSP", "RTT12"],
    sarg: {
      regime_class: "R3",
      gradient_profile: "medium"
    },
    benchmarks: {
      stability_score: "borderline",
      drift_index: 4,
      regime_thresholds: { r3_min: 3, r3_max: 7 }
    },
    hsp: {
      sensitivity_map: "moderate",
      micro_drift: 2,
      polarity_wobble: 1
    },
    rtt12: {
      phase: "P7",
      phase_risk_profile: "borderline",
      phase_aligned_thresholds: { p7_min: 2, p7_max: 5 }
    }
  },
  engine: {
    opacity: {
      phase: "P7",
      visibility_regime: "partial"
    },
    mode: {
      stance: "M2",
      stance_selected: "operator"
    },
    tel: {
      echo_phase: "E3",
      resolution_state: "in_progress"
    }
  },
  propagation_contract: {
    must_preserve: [
      "timestamp",
      "module",
      "event_type",
      "echo_transition"
    ],
    must_emit: [
      "regime_class",
      "stability_score",
      "sensitivity_map",
      "phase"
    ]
  }
};

// Run validation
const valid = validate(example);

if (valid) {
  console.log("Unified object is VALID against lens_rfr_engine.schema.json");
} else {
  console.error("Validation FAILED");
  console.error(validate.errors);
}
```

You can drop this into `scripts/validate_lens_rfr_engine.mjs` and run with `node` for quick canon checks.

# ⭐ **ResonanceInspector**  
**Unified resonance inspection module using the full SARG → Benchmarks → HSP → RTT/12 lens stack.**

## **AI‑Ready Summary**  
ResonanceInspector provides a consolidated, lens‑interpreted resonance view for downstream modules. It consumes RFR events, applies the full lens stack, and exposes a triadic‑ready interpretation surface for Opacity → Mode → TEL.

---

## **Session Context**
- **Canon:** TriadicFrameworks  
- **Module:** ResonanceInspector  
- **Drift:** 0 (canonical)  
- **Coherence:** structural, operator‑first  
- **Version:** 1.0  
- **Format:** Markdown + AI metadata  
- **Front Door:** index.html  
- **Every Page:** must preserve operator grammar  
- **Audience:** operators, diagnostics, AI‑agents  

---

## **Module Identity**
ResonanceInspector is the canonical analysis module that unifies:

- **RFR (Resonance Flight Recorder)**  
- **Lens Stack (SARG → Benchmarks → HSP → RTT/12)**  
- **Triadic Engine (Opacity → Mode → TEL)**  

It provides a stable, queryable resonance‑interpretation layer for all downstream modules.

---

## **Purpose**
- Interpret raw resonance traces using the full lens stack  
- Emit a structured, triadic‑ready resonance view  
- Provide diagnostics for drift, regime, sensitivity, and phase  
- Serve as a central inspection surface for operators and AI agents  

---

## **Scope**
ResonanceInspector covers:

- RFR event ingestion  
- Lens‑stack propagation  
- Phase‑aligned interpretation  
- Engine‑ready output formatting  
- Structural diagnostics  

It does **not** perform engine transformations itself (Opacity/Mode/TEL remain separate modules).

- `emit_lens_view`

# ⭐ **AP. ResonanceInspector — Full Operator Grammar Block**

# ResonanceInspector — Operator Grammar (v1.0)

## Operator Categories
- **Primary Operators**
  - `inspect_resonance`
  - `emit_lens_view`
  - `validate_phase_alignment`

- **Secondary Operators**
  - `compare_regimes`
  - `threshold_diff`
  - `phase_shift_detect`

---

## Operator Definitions

### `inspect_resonance`
**Category:** primary  
**Input:** `resonance_trace`  
**Output:** `lens_interpreted_resonance`  
**Behavior:**  
- Applies SARG → Benchmarks → HSP → RTT/12 in canonical order  
- Preserves operator_order, triad_closure, polarity_pairing  
- Emits full lens‑stack output set  

---

### `emit_lens_view`
**Category:** primary  
**Input:** `lens_interpreted_resonance`  
**Output:** `lens_view_packet`  
**Behavior:**  
- Normalizes lens outputs into a single inspection packet  
- Ensures dimensional coherence across all lens layers  
- Provides stable interface for downstream engine modules  

---

### `validate_phase_alignment`
**Category:** primary  
**Input:** `phase_map`, `phase_aligned_thresholds`  
**Output:** `phase_alignment_status`  
**Behavior:**  
- Checks RTT/12 phase against aligned thresholds  
- Emits `aligned | borderline | misaligned`  
- Ensures compatibility with Opacity → Mode → TEL  

---

### `compare_regimes`
**Category:** secondary  
**Input:** `regime_class`, `stability_score`  
**Output:** `regime_diff`  
**Behavior:**  
- Computes structural difference between two resonance regimes  
- Used for diagnostics and drift detection  

---

### `threshold_diff`
**Category:** secondary  
**Input:** `regime_thresholds`, `phase_aligned_thresholds`  
**Output:** `threshold_delta`  
**Behavior:**  
- Computes delta between benchmark and phase thresholds  
- Emits normalized difference metric  

---

### `phase_shift_detect`
**Category:** secondary  
**Input:** `phase_map`  
**Output:** `phase_shift_event`  
**Behavior:**  
- Detects transitions between RTT/12 phases  
- Emits structural shift event for engine modules  

---

## Operator Contracts

### Must Preserve
- `operator_order`  
- `triad_closure`  
- `polarity_pairing`  

### Must Emit
- `regime_class`  
- `stability_score`  
- `sensitivity_map`  
- `phase_map`  

---

## Operator Layer Mapping
- **Operator Layer:** structural  
- **Dimensional Layer:** lens‑stack coherence  
- **Regime Layer:** drift + stability evaluation  
- **Cross‑Cutting:** phase alignment + engine readiness

# ⭐ **inspector_core.md (canonical, drop‑in‑ready)**

```markdown
# ResonanceInspector — Core

## Purpose
Provide a unified, lens‑interpreted resonance view for downstream modules.

## Inputs
- `resonance_trace` (from RFR)
- `lens_stack` (SARG → Benchmarks → HSP → RTT/12)

## Outputs
- `lens_interpreted_resonance`
- `lens_view_packet`
- `phase_alignment_status`

---

## Pipeline

### 1. Ingest
- read `resonance_trace`
- validate structural completeness
- preserve:
  - `timestamp`
  - `module`
  - `event_type`
  - `echo_transition`

### 2. Apply Lens Stack
Order:
1. SARG  
2. Benchmarks  
3. HSP  
4. RTT/12  

Each lens:
- consumes previous outputs
- emits its own structural fields
- maintains operator_order

### 3. Consolidate
- merge all lens outputs into `lens_interpreted_resonance`
- ensure:
  - triad_closure
  - polarity_pairing
  - dimensional coherence

### 4. Emit Lens View
- normalize fields into `lens_view_packet`
- include:
  - regime_class  
  - stability_score  
  - sensitivity_map  
  - phase_map  

### 5. Phase Alignment
- compare `phase_map` with `phase_aligned_thresholds`
- emit:
  - `aligned`
  - `borderline`
  - `misaligned`

---

## Operator Contracts

### Must Preserve
- operator_order  
- triad_closure  
- polarity_pairing  

### Must Emit
- regime_class  
- stability_score  
- sensitivity_map  
- phase_map  

---

## Error Conditions
- missing lens output → `lens_incomplete`
- incoherent thresholds → `threshold_mismatch`
- invalid phase transition → `phase_violation`

---

## Engine Readiness
`lens_interpreted_resonance` must satisfy:
- Opacity: visibility_regime derivable  
- Mode: stance_selected resolvable  
- TEL: echo_phase + resolution_state computable  

---

## Notes
- no semantic interpretation  
- no external dependencies  
- all outputs must be structurally stable  
```

```markdown
# AS. Drift‑Diagnostic Operator Extension — ResonanceInspector

## New Operators

### `drift_scan`
**Category:** diagnostic  
**Input:** `lens_interpreted_resonance`  
**Output:** `drift_scan_report`  
**Behavior:**  
- scans `stability_score`, `drift_index`, `micro_drift`  
- flags:
  - `no_drift`
  - `micro_drift_only`
  - `macro_drift`

---

### `drift_band_classify`
**Category:** diagnostic  
**Input:** `drift_index`  
**Output:** `drift_band`  
**Behavior:**  
- maps integer drift_index into bands:
  - `D0` (0)  
  - `D1` (1–3)  
  - `D2` (4–7)  
  - `D3` (8+)  

---

### `regime_drift_crosscheck`
**Category:** diagnostic  
**Input:** `regime_class`, `drift_band`  
**Output:** `regime_drift_status`  
**Behavior:**  
- checks compatibility of regime_class with drift_band  
- emits:
  - `regime_stable`
  - `regime_borderline`
  - `regime_fragile`

---

### `phase_drift_trace`
**Category:** diagnostic  
**Input:** `phase_map`, `drift_band`  
**Output:** `phase_drift_profile`  
**Behavior:**  
- traces drift across RTT/12 phases  
- emits structured profile:
  - `phase_low_drift`
  - `phase_mixed_drift`
  - `phase_high_drift`

---

## Drift Contracts

### Must Preserve
- `regime_class`  
- `stability_score`  
- `drift_index`  
- `phase_map`  

### Must Emit
- `drift_band`  
- `regime_drift_status`  
- `phase_drift_profile`  

---

## Diagnostic Layer Mapping
- **Drift Layer:** `drift_scan`, `drift_band_classify`  
- **Regime Layer:** `regime_drift_crosscheck`  
- **Phase Layer:** `phase_drift_trace`  
```

```markdown
# ResonanceInspector — Diagnostics

## Purpose
Provide structured diagnostics for regime, drift, sensitivity, and phase across the lens stack and engine‑readiness surface.

---

## Diagnostic Domains
- **Regime Diagnostics**
- **Drift Diagnostics**
- **Sensitivity Diagnostics**
- **Phase Diagnostics**
- **Engine‑Readiness Checks**

---

## Regime Diagnostics

### Signals
- `regime_class`
- `stability_score`
- `regime_thresholds`

### Status Codes
- `regime_stable`
- `regime_borderline`
- `regime_fragile`

### Rules
- compare `stability_score` against `regime_thresholds`
- map result into regime status
- preserve `regime_class` for all downstream checks

---

## Drift Diagnostics

### Signals
- `drift_index`
- `micro_drift`
- `polarity_wobble`

### Status Codes
- `no_drift`
- `micro_drift_only`
- `macro_drift`

### Bands
- `D0` → drift_index = 0  
- `D1` → 1–3  
- `D2` → 4–7  
- `D3` → 8+

### Rules
- compute `drift_band` from `drift_index`
- cross‑check `drift_band` with `regime_class`
- emit `regime_drift_status`

---

## Sensitivity Diagnostics

### Signals
- `sensitivity_map`
- `micro_drift`

### Status Codes
- `low_sensitivity`
- `medium_sensitivity`
- `high_sensitivity`

### Rules
- derive sensitivity level from `sensitivity_map`
- correlate with `micro_drift`
- flag high‑risk sensitivity when both are elevated

---

## Phase Diagnostics

### Signals
- `phase_map`
- `phase_risk_profile`
- `phase_aligned_thresholds`

### Status Codes
- `phase_low_risk`
- `phase_borderline`
- `phase_high_risk`

### Rules
- compare `phase_map` with `phase_aligned_thresholds`
- emit `phase_alignment_status`:
  - `aligned`
  - `borderline`
  - `misaligned`
- trace drift across phases as `phase_drift_profile`

---

## Engine‑Readiness Checks

### Required Signals
- `visibility_regime`
- `stance_selected`
- `echo_phase`
- `resolution_state`

### Readiness Codes
- `engine_ready`
- `engine_borderline`
- `engine_blocked`

### Rules
- Opacity: `visibility_regime` must be derivable from lens outputs  
- Mode: `stance_selected` must be resolvable from regime + drift  
- TEL: `echo_phase` + `resolution_state` must be consistent with phase diagnostics  

---

## Error Conditions

### Codes
- `lens_incomplete` — missing one or more lens outputs  
- `threshold_mismatch` — inconsistent regime/phase thresholds  
- `phase_violation` — invalid phase transition  
- `engine_unready` — required engine signals not satisfiable  

### Handling
- all errors must be emitted as structured fields
- no silent failure
- preserve original `resonance_trace` for audit

---

## Audit Hooks

- log:
  - `regime_drift_status`
  - `phase_alignment_status`
  - `engine_readiness`
  - all error codes
- store in:
  - `logs/flight_recorder_overlay.json`
  - `logs/validation/overlay_validation.log`
```

---
